FILE src/modules/document-archive/application/document-archive-service.ts
  829:    * by the caller, never derived; `status` is computed immediately from it (with no evidence
  830:    * linked yet, `applicability=APPLICABLE` always starts at MISSING, `NOT_APPLICABLE` always
  831:    * starts at NOT_APPLICABLE ƒ?" `deriveRequirementStatus` is authoritative even at creation time
  832:    * rather than hardcoding the obvious no-evidence case separately).
  833:    */
  834:   async createRequirement(ctx: RequestContext, input: CreateRequirementInput): Promise<Requirement> {
  835:     authorize({ context: ctx, action: "docarchive:requirement-create", resource: { tenantId: ctx.tenant.tenantId } });
  836:     const tenantId = ctx.tenant.tenantId;
  837:     const requirementId = this.ids.newRequirementId();
  838:     const now = this.now();
  839:     // P0.1/¶õ3: `putIfAbsent` replaced by a real transaction, planned by `buildCreateRequirementEntries()`
  840:     // (D-192 ¶õ6 slice 3). Two properties this buys, neither of which a single conditional Put can
  841:     // give: the per-Subject name uniqueness rule is enforced by the pointer's own
  842:     // `attribute_not_exists` (not by a read-then-write), and the Subject's existence/status is
  843:     // fenced INSIDE the same transaction ƒ?" a pre-existing gap found by this decision's Round 2
  844:     // (`createRequirement` never checked the Subject at all and would happily create a
  845:     // Requirement under a non-existent or ARCHIVED Subject).
  846:     const { requirement, entries, labels } = buildCreateRequirementEntries({
  847:       tableName: this.tableName,
  848:       tenantId,
  849:       requirementId,
  850:       subjectId: input.subjectId,
  851:       name: input.name,
  852:       notes: input.notes,
  853:       applicability: input.applicability,
  854:       now,
  855:     });
  856:     try {
  857:       await executeTenantBusinessMutation({ store: this.store, tableName: this.tableName, tenantId, entries });
  858:     } catch (err) {
  859:       this.throwClassifiedCancellation(err, labels, { subjectId: input.subjectId, requirementId });
  860:     }
  861:     return requirement;
  862:   }
  863: 
  864:   /** Shared by `createRequirement` and `applyTemplate` ƒ?" the pointer row that makes the
  865:    * per-Subject name uniqueness rule transactional rather than a read-then-write. */
  866:   private buildRequirementNamePointer(tenantId: string, subjectId: string, name: string, requirementId: string, now: string): RequirementNamePointer {
  867:     const normalizedName = normalizeDisplayName(name);
  868:     return {
  869:       ...requirementNamePointerKey(tenantId, subjectId, normalizedName),
  870:       entityType: "RequirementNamePointer",
  871:       tenantId,
  872:       subjectId,
  873:       normalizedName,
  874:       requirementId,
  875:       createdAt: now,
  876:       updatedAt: now,
  877:       version: 1,
  878:     };
  879:   }
  880: 
  881:   /** `attribute_exists(PK) AND status = ACTIVE` ƒ?" the status is ENUMERATED, never `<> DELETED`,
  882:    * which would let an ARCHIVED Subject through (`TrackedSubjectStatus` is
  883:    * `ACTIVE | ARCHIVED | DELETED`). */
  884:   private buildSubjectFence(tenantId: string, subjectId: string) {
  885:     return buildExistenceConditionCheck({
  886:       tableName: this.tableName,
  887:       key: trackedSubjectKeyForFence(tenantId, subjectId),
  888:       extra: { status: SUBJECT_STATUS_ACCEPTING_REQUIREMENTS },
  889:     });
  890:   }
  891: 
  892:   /**
  893:    * Structural classification of a `TransactionCanceledException` ƒ?" never a literal index
  894:    * (`codes?.[1]`), which breaks the moment an entry is inserted, and never a re-read that picks
  895:    * WHICH error to throw (that would assert a cause DynamoDB did not reveal; Codex Rounds 3/4).
  896:    *
  897:    * Coupling with the shared lane, declared rather than assumed: `executeTenantBusinessMutation`
  898:    * always appends its tenant fence LAST and converts that fence's failure into
  899:    * `TenantNotActiveError` before the caller ever sees the cancellation, so `labels` covers the
  900:    * whole space this classifier can still be asked about. Any `ConditionalCheckFailed` at an
  901:    * index beyond `labels` falls back to a generic conflict rather than a wrong label.
  902:    *
  903:    * Precedence runs from the broadest precondition to the narrowest: if the template is no
  904:    * longer applicable, reporting "name X collided" would be misleading; likewise for the Subject.
  905:    */
  906:   private throwClassifiedCancellation(err: unknown, labels: TransactEntryLabel[], details: Record<string, unknown>): never {
  907:     if (!isTransactionCanceled(err)) throw err;
  908:     const codes = getCancellationReasonCodes(err);
  909:     const failed = (codes ?? []).flatMap((code, index) => (code === "ConditionalCheckFailed" && index < labels.length ? [labels[index] as TransactEntryLabel] : []));
  910: 
  911:     if (failed.some((label) => label.kind === "TEMPLATE_FENCE")) {
  912:       throw new TemplatePreconditionFailedError(undefined, details);
  913:     }
  914:     if (failed.some((label) => label.kind === "DOCUMENT_TYPE_FENCE")) {
  915:       throw new DocumentTypeNotActiveError(undefined, details);
  916:     }
  917:     if (failed.some((label) => label.kind === "SUBJECT_FENCE")) {
  918:       throw new SubjectPreconditionFailedError(undefined, details);
  919:     }
  920:     const collisions = failed.filter((label) => label.kind === "POINTER");
  921:     if (collisions.length > 0) {
  922:       // Every colliding name, not just the first ƒ?" a single apply can lose more than one race.
  923:       throw new RequirementNameConflictError(undefined, { ...details, conflictingNames: collisions.map((label) => label.name) });
  924:     }
  925:     if (failed.some((label) => label.kind === "REQUIREMENT")) {
  926:       throw new ConflictError("Requirement was concurrently modified.", details);
  927:     }
  928:     if (failed.some((label) => label.kind === "DOCUMENT")) {
  929:       throw new ConflictError("Document already exists.", details);
  930:     }
  931:     throw new ConflictError("The transaction was rejected by a condition check.", details);
  932:   }
  933: 
  934:   /** Shared by all 4 Requirement mutation sites (D-179/D-185) ƒ?" computes the GSI8 pointer fields
  935:    * to `set` (SATISFIED + `evidenceValidUntil`) or the sentinel meaning "remove instead" (every
  936:    * other case), keeping `deriveRequirementMaintenanceDue` the single source of truth for
  937:    * eligibility rather than re-deriving it at each call site. */
  938:   private requirementGsi8Fields(status: RequirementStatus, evidenceValidUntil: string | undefined, tenantId: string, requirementId: string): { GSI8PK: string; GSI8SK: string } | Record<string, never> {
  939:     const due = deriveRequirementMaintenanceDue(status, evidenceValidUntil);
  940:     return due ? requirementGsi8Keys({ dueAtIso: due.dueAtIso, tenantId, requirementId }) : {};
  941:   }
  942: 
  943:   async getRequirement(ctx: RequestContext, subjectId: string, requirementId: string): Promise<Requirement> {
  944:     authorize({ context: ctx, action: "docarchive:requirement-read", resource: { tenantId: ctx.tenant.tenantId } });
  945:     return this.getRequirementUnchecked(ctx.tenant.tenantId, subjectId, requirementId);
  946:   }
  947: 
  948:   private async getRequirementUnchecked(tenantId: string, subjectId: string, requirementId: string): Promise<Requirement> {
  949:     const requirement = await this.store.get<Requirement>(requirementKey(tenantId, subjectId, requirementId));
  950:     if (!requirement) throw new NotFoundError("Requirement not found.", { requirementId });
  951:     return requirement;
  952:   }
  953: 
  954:   async listRequirements(ctx: RequestContext, subjectId: string): Promise<Requirement[]> {
  955:     authorize({ context: ctx, action: "docarchive:requirement-read", resource: { tenantId: ctx.tenant.tenantId } });
  956:     return this.store.queryByPk<Requirement>(`TENANT#${ctx.tenant.tenantId}#SUBJECT#${subjectId}`, REQUIREMENT_SK_PREFIX);
  957:   }
  958: 
  959:   /** Never touches `evidenceVersionId`/`status` directly ƒ?" an `applicability` change here still
  960:    * re-derives `status` (e.g. flipping to NOT_APPLICABLE must immediately reflect in GSI1's
  961:    * REQSTATUS namespace, not wait for the next unrelated mutation or the daily reindex). */
  962:   async updateRequirement(ctx: RequestContext, subjectId: string, requirementId: string, expectedVersion: number, input: UpdateRequirementInput): Promise<Requirement> {
  963:     authorize({ context: ctx, action: "docarchive:requirement-update", resource: { tenantId: ctx.tenant.tenantId } });
  964:     const tenantId = ctx.tenant.tenantId;
  965:     const current = await this.getRequirementUnchecked(tenantId, subjectId, requirementId);
  966:     const nextApplicability = input.applicability ?? current.applicability;
  967:     const now = this.now();
  968:     let status = current.status;
  969:     if (nextApplicability !== current.applicability) {
  970:       const evidence = this.cachedEvidenceForDerivation(current);
  971:       status = deriveRequirementStatus(nextApplicability, evidence, new Date(now));
  972:     }
  973:     // Applicability is the only field here that can change `status` (name/notes never do), and
  974:     // `evidenceValidUntil` itself never changes in this call ƒ?" only whether the GSI8 pointer
  975:     // built from it should exist follows `status`.
  976:     const gsi8Fields = this.requirementGsi8Fields(status, current.evidenceValidUntil, tenantId, requirementId);
  977:     const set: Record<string, unknown> = { applicability: nextApplicability, status, ...requirementGsi1Keys(tenantId, status, now, requirementId), ...gsi8Fields };
  978:     if (input.name !== undefined) set["name"] = input.name;
  979:     if (input.notes !== undefined) set["notes"] = input.notes;
  980:     const update = buildVersionedUpdate({
  981:       tableName: this.tableName,
  982:       key: requirementKey(tenantId, subjectId, requirementId),
  983:       tenantId,
  984:       expectedVersion,
  985:       set,
  986:       remove: Object.keys(gsi8Fields).length === 0 ? ["GSI8PK", "GSI8SK"] : undefined,
  987:     });
  988: 
  989:     // P0.1/¶õ3: a name change has to move the dedupe pointer too, in the SAME transaction. Two
  990:     // branches for the same reason `renameDocumentType()` has two (D-173 ¶õ3): DynamoDB rejects a
  991:     // Delete and a Put against the SAME item inside one TransactWriteItems, so "normalized name
  992:     // unchanged" and "normalized name changed" cannot share one transaction shape. The old
  993:     // normalized name is ALWAYS derived from the persisted `current.name`, never from caller
  994:     // input ƒ?" the persisted value is the only source of truth for which pointer key exists.
  995:     const oldNormalizedName = normalizeDisplayName(current.name);
  996:     const newNormalizedName = input.name !== undefined ? normalizeDisplayName(input.name) : oldNormalizedName;
  997:     const entries: TransactWriteEntry[] = [{ Update: update }];
  998:     const labels: TransactEntryLabel[] = [{ kind: "REQUIREMENT" }];
  999:     if (newNormalizedName !== oldNormalizedName) {
 1000:       entries.push({
 1001:         Delete: buildConditionalDelete({
 1002:           tableName: this.tableName,
 1003:           key: requirementNamePointerKey(tenantId, subjectId, oldNormalizedName),
 1004:           conditionExpression: "attribute_exists(PK) AND #reqId = :self",
 1005:           names: { "#reqId": "requirementId" },
 1006:           values: { ":self": requirementId },
 1007:         }),
 1008:       });
 1009:       labels.push({ kind: "POINTER", name: current.name });
 1010:       entries.push({
 1011:         Put: buildVersionedCreate(
 1012:           this.tableName,
 1013:           this.buildRequirementNamePointer(tenantId, subjectId, input.name as string, requirementId, now) as unknown as Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey,
 1014:         ),
 1015:       });
 1016:       labels.push({ kind: "POINTER", name: input.name as string });
 1017:     }
 1018: 
 1019:     try {
 1020:       await executeTenantBusinessMutation({ store: this.store, tableName: this.tableName, tenantId, entries });
 1581:    * (200 with `created: []`), not a conflict ƒ?" that is the real "the customer added one item to
 1582:    * the template, apply just that one" use case.
 1583:    *
 1584:    * Known, declared window: the plan's read is eventually consistent (`queryByPk` does not use
 1585:    * `ConsistentRead`), so a Requirement created moments earlier may be missed by the plan and
 1586:    * collide at commit, cancelling the whole apply instead of being skipped. Re-applying is safe,
 1587:    * and once the read converges the item is skipped.
 1588:    */
 1589:   async applyTemplate(
 1590:     ctx: RequestContext,
 1591:     templateId: string,
 1592:     subjectId: string,
 1593:     expectedTemplateVersion?: number,
 1594:   ): Promise<{ created: Array<{ templateItemId: string; requirementId: string; name: string }>; skipped: TemplateApplicationPlan["skip"]; templateVersion: number }> {
 1595:     authorize({ context: ctx, action: "docarchive:requirementtemplate-apply", resource: { tenantId: ctx.tenant.tenantId } });
 1596:     const tenantId = ctx.tenant.tenantId;
 1597:     const template = await this.getRequirementTemplateUnchecked(tenantId, templateId);
 1598:     const fencedVersion = expectedTemplateVersion ?? template.version;
 1599:     const existing = await this.readExistingForPlan(tenantId, subjectId);
 1600:     const plan = planTemplateApplication(template.items, existing);
 1601: 
 1602:     if (plan.create.length === 0) {
 1603:       // Nothing to write. Deliberately NOT a transaction with only fences: the lane rejects a
 1604:       // zero-mutation call, and there is no state to protect when nothing is being written.
 1605:       return { created: [], skipped: plan.skip, templateVersion: fencedVersion };
 1606:     }
 1607: 
 1608:     const now = this.now();
 1609:     const entries: TransactWriteEntry[] = [];
 1610:     const labels: TransactEntryLabel[] = [];
 1611:     const created: Array<{ templateItemId: string; requirementId: string; name: string }> = [];
 1612: 
 1613:     for (const item of plan.create) {
 1614:       const requirementId = this.ids.newRequirementId();
 1615:       const status = deriveRequirementStatus(item.applicability, undefined, new Date(now));
 1616:       const requirement: Requirement = {
 1617:         ...requirementKey(tenantId, subjectId, requirementId),
 1618:         entityType: "Requirement",
 1619:         requirementId,
 1620:         tenantId,
 1621:         subjectId,
 1622:         name: item.name,
 1623:         ...(item.notes !== undefined ? { notes: item.notes } : {}),
 1624:         applicability: item.applicability,
 1625:         status,
 1626:         createdAt: now,
 1627:         updatedAt: now,
 1628:         version: 1,
 1629:         ...requirementGsi1Keys(tenantId, status, now, requirementId),
 1630:         ...this.requirementGsi8Fields(status, undefined, tenantId, requirementId),
 1631:         // Provenance ONLY ƒ?" no read path, derivation or worker ever consults these three.
 1632:         sourceTemplateId: templateId,
 1633:         sourceTemplateItemId: item.templateItemId,
 1634:         sourceTemplateAppliedVersion: fencedVersion,
 1635:       };
 1636:       entries.push({ Put: buildVersionedCreate(this.tableName, requirement as unknown as Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey) });
 1637:       labels.push({ kind: "REQUIREMENT", templateItemId: item.templateItemId });
 1638:       entries.push({
 1639:         Put: buildVersionedCreate(this.tableName, this.buildRequirementNamePointer(tenantId, subjectId, item.name, requirementId, now) as unknown as Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey),
 1640:       });
 1641:       labels.push({ kind: "POINTER", name: item.name, templateItemId: item.templateItemId });

    1: /**
    2:  * DocumentVersion ƒ?" D-143 Decision 1 (state machine) + Decision 6 (file lifecycle).
    3:  * `DocumentVersion.state` is the SOLE mutable source of truth (Decision 3) ƒ?" the append-only
    4:  * `DocumentVersionEvent` log (`document-version-event.ts`) records every transition but never
    5:  * competes with this field for authority.
    6:  *
    7:  * Graph (estado-final-consolidado.md Decision 1):
    8:  *   (none)      -[reserveUpload]->        DRAFT
    9:  *   DRAFT       -[commitUpload]->         RECEIVED
   10:  *   DRAFT       -[abandonUpload]->        WITHDRAWN
   11:  *   RECEIVED    -[claimReview]->          UNDER_REVIEW
   12:  *   RECEIVED | UNDER_REVIEW -[acceptVersion]-> ACCEPTED
   13:  *   RECEIVED | UNDER_REVIEW -[rejectVersion]-> REJECTED
   14:  *   ACCEPTED    -[superseded by another version's acceptVersion]-> SUPERSEDED
   15:  *   UNDER_REVIEW -[claim TTL sweeper]->    RECEIVED
   16:  *
   17:  * REJECTED is a terminal state that is NEVER removable (D-143 Decision 7 ƒ?" a Rodada 1 proposal
   18:  * that allowed removing REJECTED versions was rejected for directly contradicting J9's
   19:  * "the rejected file stays in history" requirement). Only DRAFT can ever be physically removed
   20:  * (via WITHDRAWN, before it ever became evidence).
   21:  */

   23: 
   24: export type DocumentVersionState = "DRAFT" | "RECEIVED" | "UNDER_REVIEW" | "ACCEPTED" | "REJECTED" | "SUPERSEDED" | "WITHDRAWN";
   25: 
   26: /** Closed taxonomy, `document-domain-functional-specification-v0.1.md` ¶õ12. */
   27: export type RejectionReason = "EXPIRED" | "ILLEGIBLE" | "INCORRECT" | "WRONG_SUBJECT" | "OUTDATED_VERSION" | "INCOMPLETE" | "OTHER";
   28: 
   29: /** Origin taxonomy, spec ¶õ13. */

   48:    * alone is NOT sufficient (a version with zero pending and one INFECTED file must never be
   49:    * accepted), both must be zero. */
   50:   pendingFileScans: number;
   51:   infectedFileScans: number;
   52:   /** D-163 ¶õ2: set atomically by `reserveFiles()`'s single `TransactWriteItems`, together with

   56:    * tried to close with input validation alone (D-163 ¶õ2, achado real da Rodada 1). Once
   57:    * `true`, no further file reservation against this Version is possible ƒ?" the only recovery
   58:    * for a bad file set is rejecting the whole Version and starting a new one (D-143

   69:    * index only ever contains live review work (AWS DynamoDB sparse-index pattern). */

   71:   GSI5SK?: string;
   72: }

   76:   async queryGsi7<T extends EntityKey = Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey>(input: Gsi7QueryInput): Promise<T[]> {
   77:     try {
   78:       const items: T[] = [];
   79:       let exclusiveStartKey: Record<string, unknown> | undefined;
   80:       do {
   81:         const result = await this.client.send(
   82:           new QueryCommand({
   83:             TableName: this.tableName,
   84:             IndexName: "GSI7",
   85:             KeyConditionExpression: "GSI7PK = :pk",
   86:             ExpressionAttributeValues: { ":pk": input.gsi7pk },
   87:             ScanIndexForward: input.ascending ?? true,
   88:             Limit: input.limit,
   89:             ExclusiveStartKey: exclusiveStartKey,
   90:           }),
   91:         );
   92:         items.push(...((result.Items ?? []) as T[]));
   93:         exclusiveStartKey = result.LastEvaluatedKey;
   94:         if (input.limit && items.length >= input.limit) break;
   95:       } while (exclusiveStartKey);
   96:       return input.limit ? items.slice(0, input.limit) : items;
   97:     } catch (err) {
   98:       throw mapDynamoError(err, "SubjectStore.queryGsi7");
   99:     }
  100:   }
  101: 
  102:   async queryByPk<T extends EntityKey = Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey>(pk: string, skPrefix?: string): Promise<T[]> {
  103:     try {
  104:       const items: T[] = [];
  105:       let exclusiveStartKey: Record<string, unknown> | undefined;
  106:       do {
  107:         const result = await this.client.send(
  108:           new QueryCommand({
  109:             TableName: this.tableName,
  110:             KeyConditionExpression: skPrefix ? "PK = :pk AND begins_with(SK, :prefix)" : "PK = :pk",
  111:             ExpressionAttributeValues: skPrefix ? { ":pk": pk, ":prefix": skPrefix } : { ":pk": pk },
  112:             ExclusiveStartKey: exclusiveStartKey,
  113:           }),
  114:         );
  115:         items.push(...((result.Items ?? []) as T[]));
  116:         exclusiveStartKey = result.LastEvaluatedKey;
  117:       } while (exclusiveStartKey);
  118:       return items;
  119:     } catch (err) {
  120:       throw mapDynamoError(err, "SubjectStore.queryByPk");
  121:     }
  122:   }
  123: 
  124:   /** D-192 ¶õ4 ƒ?" `BatchGetItem` real, 100 chaves por chamada (limite do SDK), retry de
  125:    * `UnprocessedKeys` atÇ¸ esvaziar (mesma disciplina de retry que `client.ts` jÇ­ aplica a nÇðvel
  126:    * de conexÇœo via `maxAttempts`, mas `UnprocessedKeys` nÇœo Ç¸ uma falha de rede - o SDK devolve
  127:    * 200 OK com uma sublista nÇœo processada, entÇœo precisa de retry explÇðcito aqui). */
  128:   async batchGet<T extends EntityKey = Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey>(keys: EntityKey[]): Promise<T[]> {
  129:     if (keys.length === 0) return [];
  130:     try {
  131:       const items: T[] = [];
  132:       const CHUNK_SIZE = 100;
  133:       for (let i = 0; i < keys.length; i += CHUNK_SIZE) {
  134:         let pending = keys.slice(i, i + CHUNK_SIZE);
  135:         while (pending.length > 0) {
  136:           const result = await this.client.send(
  137:             new BatchGetCommand({
  138:               RequestItems: { [this.tableName]: { Keys: pending as unknown as Record<string, unknown>[] } },
  139:             }),
  140:           );
  141:           items.push(...((result.Responses?.[this.tableName] ?? []) as T[]));
  142:           const unprocessed = result.UnprocessedKeys?.[this.tableName]?.Keys;
  143:           pending = (unprocessed ?? []) as unknown as EntityKey[];
  144:         }
  145:       }
  146:       return items;
  147:     } catch (err) {
  148:       throw mapDynamoError(err, "SubjectStore.batchGet");
  149:     }
  150:   }
  151: }

   82:     } catch (err) {

   87:   /** D-136/D-E: one real QueryCommand per call - never an internal accumulate-across-pages
   88:    * loop (the pre-D-136 version did that and then `slice(0, limit)`'d the result, so its
   89:    * returned `LastEvaluatedKey` could point past items the sliced-off result never actually
   90:    * returned to the caller - a real cursor-skip bug found in the D-136/D-E protocol). A
   91:    * DynamoDB `Query` can stop on `Limit` OR the 1 MB response-size ceiling, whichever comes
   92:    * first; `LastEvaluatedKey` always reflects the actual stopping point of THIS call, so a
   93:    * page's own cursor is always correct to resume from, regardless of which limit stopped it. */
   94:   async queryGsi1Page<T extends EntityKey = Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey>(input: Gsi1PageInput): Promise<Gsi1Page<T>> {
   95:     try {
   96:       const result = await this.client.send(
   97:         new QueryCommand({
   98:           TableName: this.tableName,
   99:           IndexName: "GSI1",
  100:           KeyConditionExpression: "GSI1PK = :pk",
  101:           ExpressionAttributeValues: { ":pk": input.gsi1pk },
  102:           ScanIndexForward: input.ascending ?? true,
  103:           Limit: input.limit,
  104:           ExclusiveStartKey: input.exclusiveStartKey,
  105:         }),
  106:       );
  107:       return { items: (result.Items ?? []) as T[], lastEvaluatedKey: result.LastEvaluatedKey };
  108:     } catch (err) {
  109:       throw mapDynamoError(err, "ExpirationStore.queryGsi1Page");
  110:     }
  111:   }

/**
 * AssignedActiveItemsLookup ƒ?" D-122 (Responsibility Reassignment on Member Removal,
 * `docs/architecture/reviews/responsibility-reassignment-scoping/`, Claude 9.1/Codex 9.1, 3
 * rounds). Round-1 named this `SoleResponsibilityChecker`/`findSoleResponsibilityItems`; Round-3
 * ("Estado final consolidado") superseded that name/shape and is authoritative.
 *
 * A narrow port in the CONSUMING module (`organization`), never a direct import of
 * `expiration`'s domain/persistence internals ƒ?" the real implementation lives in the composition
 * root (`runtime/aws/composition/organization.ts`), querying GSI1 directly there. This is the
 * FIRST time `organization` needs to read data owned by `expiration` ƒ?" the reverse direction of
 * `expiration/ports/member-eligibility.ts` (that port lets `expiration` read `organization`'s
 * Membership/GlobalUser data; this one lets `organization` read `expiration`'s ExpirationItem
 * data) ƒ?" same structural pattern, opposite module direction.
 *
 * Pagination contract (Round-3 CorreÇõÇœo 2 ƒ?" a real bug class the Round-2 Codex critique caught):
 * `Query`/`Limit` in DynamoDB bounds items EVALUATED before the `FilterExpression`, not items
 * that SURVIVE it ƒ?" a naive `Limit: 20` could report "only 3 found" when more exist past the
 * unevaluated 21st raw item. Implementations of this port MUST page to exhaustion (follow
 * `LastEvaluatedKey` until `undefined`, same discipline already in production for GSI1/GSI3/GSI6
 * reads elsewhere in this codebase) and apply the 20-item cap ONLY to the returned `itemIds`
 * list, computed AFTER counting the true total. `totalKnown` is the real count of matches across
 * every page scanned; `truncated` is `true` only when `totalKnown > itemIds.length`. Never use
 * `Limit` as a truncation proxy ƒ?" that would produce false negatives (a caller could conclude
 * "no reassignment needed" when items actually exist beyond the unevaluated boundary).
 *
 * Cost note (accepted, not a defect to fix here): RCU cost of a full page-to-exhaustion scan
 * scales with the tenant's ACTIVE-status partition size on GSI1 ƒ?" a known, accepted limitation at
 * this project's current stage (no real production tenants yet, Round-3 "Estado final
 * consolidado").
 */
export interface AssignedActiveItemsResult {
  /** Up to 20 of the matching ExpirationItem ids ƒ?" never the full set when `truncated`. */
  itemIds: string[];
  /** The TRUE count of ACTIVE items assigned to `userId`, counted across every page scanned ƒ?"
   * never derived from (and never capped by) `itemIds.length`. */
  totalKnown: number;
  /** `true` only when `totalKnown > itemIds.length` (i.e. more matches exist than the 20-item
   * cap returns). */
  truncated: boolean;
}

export interface AssignedActiveItemsLookup {
  findAssignedActiveItems(organizationId: string, userId: string): Promise<AssignedActiveItemsResult>;
}
    this.name = "OrganizationClosureUnavailableError";
  }
}

/** D-122/D-125 (Responsibility Reassignment on Member Removal): thrown by
 * `RemoveMembershipService.remove()`/`LeaveOrganizationService.leave()` when `targetUserId` is
 * still `assigneeUserId` of at least one `ACTIVE` `ExpirationItem` in the organization
 * (`AssignedActiveItemsLookup`, `organization/ports/assigned-active-items-lookup.ts`). Explicitly
 * best-effort, NOT atomic (Round-3 "Estado final consolidado") - a synchronous read-then-decide
 * precondition before the removal transaction, not part of it; a rare concurrent reassignment
 * between the check and the transaction remains a residual, observed only via the existing
 * `MEMBER_REMOVED`/`MEMBER_LEFT` audit trail and B2B-11's notification-cancellation log, never a
 * new telemetry mechanism invented just for this case. `itemIds` is capped at 20 per
 * `AssignedActiveItemsLookup`'s pagination contract; `totalKnown`/`truncated` always reflect the
 * true count. `retryable: false`: the caller must reassign the flagged items first (via the
 * existing `updateItem` mutation, already validated by `MemberEligibilityChecker`), not just
 * retry the identical removal. */
export class ResponsibilityReassignmentRequiredError extends AppError {
  constructor(
    input: { targetUserId: string; itemIds: string[]; totalKnown: number; truncated: boolean },
    message = "Target user is still the assignee of active expiration items - reassign them before removing this member.",
  ) {
    super({
      code: "RESPONSIBILITY_REASSIGNMENT_REQUIRED",
      category: "BUSINESS_RULE",
      message,
      retryable: false,
      details: { ...input },
    });
    this.name = "ResponsibilityReassignmentRequiredError";
  }
}

/** D-173 (`DocumentType` catalog, `docs/architecture/reviews/document-type-scoping/
 * estado-final-consolidado.md` ¶õ3): thrown when `createDocumentType()`'s pointer `Put` or
 * `renameDocumentType()`'s changed-name pointer `Put` loses the dedupe race ƒ?" the target
 * normalized name is already claimed by a different `DocumentType`. `retryable: false`: the
 * caller must pick a different name, not just retry the identical request. */
export class DocumentTypeNameConflictError extends AppError {
  constructor(message = "A DocumentType with this name already exists.", details?: Record<string, unknown>) {

/**
 * Pure status-derivation function (Decision 5) ƒ?" no I/O, unit-testable in isolation, same
 * style/discipline as `document-version.ts`'s `assertValidDocumentVersionTransition`.
 *
 * Branch order matters and is deliberately fail-closed-toward-MISSING/NOT_SATISFIED:
 *  1. `applicability = NOT_APPLICABLE` always wins ƒ?" status is NOT_APPLICABLE regardless of
 *     any evidence link (Decision 5: applicability is a persisted fact, evidence never
 *     overrides it).
 *  2. No evidence linked at all -> MISSING.
 *  3. Evidence linked but its DocumentVersion has not reached ACCEPTED yet (still DRAFT/
 *     RECEIVED/UNDER_REVIEW, or terminal-but-not-accepted like REJECTED/WITHDRAWN/SUPERSEDED
 *     ƒ?" the latter two should never realistically be the CURRENT evidence pointer, but the
 *     function stays total rather than assuming a caller-side invariant) -> PENDING.
 *  4. Evidence ACCEPTED, no `validUntil` (document doesn't expire) or `validUntil` still in
 *     the future (`>= now`, not `> now` ƒ?" a document valid through end-of-day today is not
 *     yet expired) -> SATISFIED.
 *  5. Evidence ACCEPTED but `validUntil` in the past -> NOT_SATISFIED.
 */
export function deriveRequirementStatus(
  applicability: RequirementApplicability,
  evidenceVersion: EvidenceVersionForDerivation | undefined,
  now: Date,
): RequirementStatus {
  if (applicability === "NOT_APPLICABLE") return "NOT_APPLICABLE";
  if (!evidenceVersion) return "MISSING";
  if (evidenceVersion.state !== "ACCEPTED") return "PENDING";
  if (!evidenceVersion.validUntil) return "SATISFIED";
  return new Date(evidenceVersion.validUntil).getTime() >= now.getTime() ? "SATISFIED" : "NOT_SATISFIED";
}

/** Same "vence em breve" window as `frontend/src/api/presentation.ts`'s `SOON_THRESHOLD_DAYS`
 * ƒ?" kept as an independent constant (not imported across the backend/frontend boundary,
 * which this codebase never crosses) but MUST be changed together with that one if the
 * product decision behind the 7-day window ever changes. */
const EXPIRING_SOON_THRESHOLD_DAYS = 7;

/** D9: EXPIRING is a read-time subdivision of SATISFIED, never persisted. Only meaningful
 * when `status === "SATISFIED"` and a `validUntil` exists ƒ?" returns `false` for every other
 * status (including a SATISFIED requirement whose evidence has no `validUntil` at all: a
 * document with no expiration can never be "about to expire"). */
export function isRequirementExpiringSoon(status: RequirementStatus, validUntil: string | undefined, now: Date): boolean {
  if (status !== "SATISFIED" || !validUntil) return false;
  const daysUntil = Math.ceil((new Date(validUntil).getTime() - now.getTime()) / (1000 * 60 * 60 * 24));
  return daysUntil >= 0 && daysUntil <= EXPIRING_SOON_THRESHOLD_DAYS;
}

/** GSI8 namespace for `requirement-reindex` (D-179/D-185, 4th of 9 MaintenanceDueIndex
 * migrations) ƒ?" see the module doc comment / `requirement-reindex/reindex.ts` for why this is
 * `evidenceValidUntil`, not a second "reindex schedule" concept: the daily worker's ONLY job is
 * the pure time-based SATISFIED -> NOT_SATISFIED drift, and `evidenceValidUntil` compared against
 * `now` is exactly that `dueAt` (D-179 Round 3's correction). */
export const REQUIREMENT_REINDEX_WORK_TYPE = "REQUIREMENT_REINDEX";

/** Due only while `status === "SATISFIED"` AND an `evidenceValidUntil` exists ƒ?" a SATISFIED
 * requirement whose evidence never expires (no `validUntil`) is SATISFIED forever, by
 * construction, and never needs a reindex. Every other status is never a candidate (MISSING/
 * PENDING/NOT_SATISFIED/NOT_APPLICABLE do not drift on their own ƒ?" some other explicit mutation
 * is always the trigger for those). */
export function deriveRequirementMaintenanceDue(status: RequirementStatus, evidenceValidUntil: string | undefined): { dueAtIso: string } | undefined {
  if (status !== "SATISFIED" || !evidenceValidUntil) return undefined;
  return { dueAtIso: evidenceValidUntil };
}

/** `GSI8SK` embeds `requirementId` (not `subjectId`) for uniqueness ƒ?" the candidate source
 * recovers `tenantId`/`subjectId`/`requirementId` from the base table's own `PK`/`SK`
 * (`requirementKey()`'s shape), same "KEYS_ONLY already returns them for free" posture as
 * `documentFileGsi8Keys()`. */
export function requirementGsi8Keys(input: { dueAtIso: string; tenantId: string; requirementId: string }): { GSI8PK: string; GSI8SK: string } {
  return {
    GSI8PK: `WORK#${REQUIREMENT_REINDEX_WORK_TYPE}`,
    GSI8SK: `${input.dueAtIso}#TENANT#${input.tenantId}#REQUIREMENT#${input.requirementId}`,
  };
}

/**
 * GSI_EVIDENCE (D-193 slice 5, physical GSI9): sparse reverse index from an evidence
 * `DocumentVersion` back to every `Requirement` that currently links it ƒ?" `linkEvidence`
 * SETs these two attributes, `unlinkEvidence` REMOVEs them (genuinely absent afterward, never
 * set-to-null), same "removed field, not nulled field" discipline as GSI8's pointer above.
 * `GSI9SK` embeds `requirementId` for uniqueness (a Subject's Requirements never collide, but two
 * DIFFERENT Subjects' Requirements can legitimately reference the same evidence versionId, e.g.
 * two Requirements pointing at one shared certificate ƒ?" the SK must disambiguate those, not just
 * be a constant marker). Deliberately NOT keyed by `subjectId` ƒ?" the whole point of this index is
 * "which Requirement(s) reference this DocumentVersion", found from the DocumentVersion side,
/**
 * DynamoDB surface the document-archive module needs ƒ?" same SDK-agnostic port pattern as
 * `src/modules/expiration/ports/expiration-store.ts` (M1's `IdentityStore` precedent):
 * callers inject an implementation, so domain/application logic stays unit-testable without
 * live AWS. Imports the shared TransactWriteItems builders/entry shapes directly from
 * `shared/dynamodb/occ.ts` (never from another module's port) ƒ?" the exact cross-module
 * ports->ports dependency the 2026-08-19 Engineering Maturity Review moved these out of
 * `expiration-store.ts` to avoid.
 *
 * D-143 Decision 2's `acceptVersion` transaction (up to 10 actions across Document/
 * DocumentVersion/DocumentVersionEvent/Requirement/DocumentRequest) is built by the
 * application layer using these same shared builders ƒ?" this port only executes whatever
 * `TransactWriteEntry[]` it is handed, it does not know about `acceptVersion` itself.
 */
import type { EntityKey, TransactWriteEntry } from "../../../shared/dynamodb/occ.js";

export type { EntityKey, TransactWriteEntry };
export { TRANSACTION_CANCELED, isTransactionCanceled } from "../../../shared/dynamodb/occ.js";

/**
 * Generic paginated GSI query ƒ?" this module spans 3 different indexes (GSI1 discriminated by
 * prefix for Document/Requirement-by-status, GSI2 for Document-by-Subject and Request-by-
 * status+responsible, GSI5 for the review queue and version lookup ƒ?" D-143 Decision 2), so a
 * single `indexName`-parameterized method replaces `ExpirationStore`'s GSI1-only
 * `queryGsi1Page` rather than duplicating three near-identical methods. One real physical
 * `QueryCommand` per call, never an internal accumulate-then-slice loop (the exact cursor-skip
 * bug D-142 found and fixed for `ExpirationStore` ƒ?" this module inherits that lesson rather
 * than reintroducing it).
 */
export interface IndexPageInput {
  /** The partition-key ATTRIBUTE NAME is derived from `indexName` by the persistence
   * implementation (GSI1 -> GSI1PK, etc.) ƒ?" never supplied by the caller, so a caller cannot
   * accidentally query GSI2 with a GSI1PK-shaped value. */
  indexName: "GSI1" | "GSI2" | "GSI5" | "GSI9";
  partitionKeyValue: string;
  ascending?: boolean;
  limit?: number;
  exclusiveStartKey?: Record<string, unknown>;
}
export interface IndexPage<T> {
  items: T[];
  lastEvaluatedKey?: Record<string, unknown>;
}

/** One page of a cross-tenant `Scan` (see `scanSatisfiedRequirements`'s doc comment). */
export interface ScanPage<T> {
  items: T[];
  lastEvaluatedKey?: Record<string, unknown>;
}

export interface DocumentArchiveStore {
  /** Strongly consistent single-item read ƒ?" every read this module does gates a mutation
   * (review decisions, accept/reject) or serves an authenticated detail view, never a
   * best-effort dashboard render (D-143 Decision 2 keeps `ConsistentRead` on throughout,
   * same posture D-136 required for session/identity reads). */
  get<T extends EntityKey = Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey>(key: EntityKey): Promise<T | undefined>;
  /** PutItem conditioned on attribute_not_exists(PK) AND attribute_not_exists(SK) ƒ?" true if
   * this call created the item, false if it already existed (used for Document/DocumentVersion
   * creation and for idempotency-record writes outside a larger transaction). */
  putIfAbsent<T extends EntityKey>(item: T): Promise<boolean>;
  /** PutItem conditioned on a counter still matching `expected` at write time ƒ?" same
   * lost-update guard `IdentityStore.updateConditional`/`SubjectStore.updateConditional`
   * already established (production bug precedent: `count` is a DynamoDB reserved word,
   * requires `ExpressionAttributeNames`). D-146 (guest access): used by
   * `DocumentArchiveGuestRateLimiter`'s fixed-window counter. */
  updateConditional<T extends EntityKey>(item: T, expected: { count: number; resetAt: string }): Promise<boolean>;
  /** Commits every entry atomically; throws an error recognized by `isTransactionCanceled()`
   * if ANY entry's ConditionExpression fails ƒ?" callers must never assume partial application.
   * This is how `acceptVersion`/`rejectVersion`/`materializeAttempt` (D-143 Decisions 1/2/8)
   * are actually executed. */
  transactWrite(entries: TransactWriteEntry[]): Promise<void>;
  /** Query the Document's own partition (Document/DocumentVersion/DocumentFile/
   * DocumentVersionEvent all co-located under `PK=TENANT#<t>#DOCUMENT#<id>`, D-143 Decision 2
   * AP1/AP2/AP9/AP10 ƒ?" no GSI needed for any of these). */
  queryByPk<T extends EntityKey = Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey>(pk: string, skPrefix?: string): Promise<T[]>;
  /** Eventually consistent GSI query, one physical page per call. */
  queryIndexPage<T extends EntityKey = Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey>(input: IndexPageInput): Promise<IndexPage<T>>;
  /**
   * Cross-tenant `Scan` filtered to `entityType = "DocumentRequestSeries" AND status = "ACTIVE"`
   * ƒ?" the recurrence materializer worker's "what's due" source (D-143 Decision 8/D-147). Same
   * accepted cost tradeoff as `scanSatisfiedRequirements` (this module still has no
   * tenant-enumeration port method, so a GSI1 query keyed by a specific `TENANT#<t>#...`
   * partition can't answer "every ACTIVE series across every tenant" without one) ƒ?" a `Scan`
   * filtered at the storage layer, not a full-index accumulate-then-filter in application code.
   * One physical `ScanCommand` per call, paginated by the caller.
   */
  scanActiveSeries<T extends EntityKey = Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey>(exclusiveStartKey?: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<ScanPage<T>>;
  /**
   * D-193 item 7/9 (`estado-final-consolidado.md`'s "Rede de reparo autoritativa") ƒ?" cross-tenant
   * `Scan` filtered to `entityType = "Requirement" AND attribute_exists(evidenceVersionId)`, the
   * SAME accepted cost tradeoff as `scanActiveSeries` above (this module still has no
   * tenant-enumeration port method). Deliberately status-independent ƒ?" filters ONLY on the
   * presence of a linked evidence version, never on `status`, because the whole point of the
   * daily sweep this feeds is to self-heal a Requirement whose STATUS might be stale precisely
   * because a `SQS_REQUIREMENT_EVIDENCE_REFRESH_V1` wake-up was lost (item 6/9) ƒ?" filtering by
   * status here would exclude the exact rows most likely to be wrong. One physical `ScanCommand`
   * per call, paginated by the caller (same shape as `scanActiveSeries`).
   */
  scanRequirementsWithEvidence<T extends EntityKey = Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey>(exclusiveStartKey?: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<ScanPage<T>>;
  /** D-192 ¶õ4 (fatia 6) ƒ?" `BatchGetItem` real, mesmo contrato de `SubjectStore.batchGet`
   * (`import/application/resolve-subject-references.ts`, fatia 5): usado pela resoluÇõÇœo
   * batched de referÇ¦ncia de DocumentType do bulk-import de Document (duas fases,
   * `DocumentTypeNamePointer` numa fase, `DocumentType` na outra). Ordem do retorno nÇœo Ç¸
   * garantida; chave ausente na tabela Ç¸ simplesmente omitida do array de retorno. */
  batchGet<T extends EntityKey = Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey>(keys: EntityKey[]): Promise<T[]>;
}
/**
 * DynamoDB surface do mÇüdulo subject ƒ?" mesmo padrÇœo SDK-agnostic de

 * TrackedSubject/RequirementAssignment que afeta o contador de entitlement precisa comitar
 * agregado + contador + audit numa Ç§nica TransactWriteItems (nunca separado, ver

 */
import type { EntityKey, TransactWriteEntry } from "../../../shared/dynamodb/occ.js";

export type { EntityKey, TransactWriteEntry };
export { TRANSACTION_CANCELED, isTransactionCanceled } from "../../../shared/dynamodb/occ.js";

  gsi7pk: string;
  ascending?: boolean;
  limit?: number;
}

  /** Leitura fortemente consistente (mesma exigÇ¦ncia de ExpirationStore.get). */

  putIfAbsent<T extends EntityKey>(item: T): Promise<boolean>;
  /** Unconditional overwrite - only for bookkeeping writes with no concurrent-writer risk (ver `updateConditional` para o caso de contador). */
  update<T extends EntityKey>(item: T): Promise<void>;
  /** PutItem condicionado ao contador ainda bater com `expected` no momento da escrita ƒ?" mesmo

   * Ç¸ palavra reservada do DynamoDB, exige `ExpressionAttributeNames`). Usado por

   * novo (coleÇõÇœo sob a partiÇõÇœo, mesmo padrÇœo de identity/document jÇ­ em produÇõÇœo). */
  queryByPk<T extends EntityKey = Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey>(pk: string, skPrefix?: string): Promise<T[]>;

   * referÇ¦ncia do bulk-import de Document/Requirement para resolver um `Set` de chaves

  batchGet<T extends EntityKey = Record<string, unknown> & EntityKey>(keys: EntityKey[]): Promise<T[]>;

infra/modules/dynamo-table/main.tf:229:  tenant_facing_index_names = ["GSI1", "GSI2", "GSI5", "GSI7", "GSI9"]
infra/modules/dynamo-table/main.tf:232:    [for name in local.tenant_facing_index_names : "${local.table_arn}/index/${name}"],
infra/modules/dynamo-table/main.tf:261:# General read/write policy on the base table + GSI1/GSI2/GSI4/GSI5 (tenant-scoped

codex
## Veredito da Rodada 2

- **Régua: 9,6/10 — STABLE**
- **Design: 6,2/10 — NOT APPROVED**
- **Protocolo:** precisa de Rodada 3.

Concordo com a separação de “validade unificada” e “responsável” em dois critérios independentes de 15%. A soma fecha em 100%, os riscos são distintos e não há necessidade de nova reconciliação da régua.

A revisão corrigiu substancialmente a Rodada 1: a unidade discriminada é melhor, o cache de validade em `Requirement` é real, `DRAFT` foi corrigido, os cursores deixaram de fingir uma ordenação global e o mecanismo de responsável está mais completo. Porém, ainda há incompatibilidades físicas e contratos indeterminados.

## Achados bloqueantes

### 1. Prefixo de nome no GSI7 não é nativo sem `type`

A revisão afirma que `NAME#<displayNameNormalized>` é prefixo nativo do índice ([round2-claude-revision.md:60](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/docs/architecture/reviews/search-and-filters-scoping/round2-claude-revision.md:60)). Mas a chave real é:

```text
TYPE#<type>#NAME#<displayNameNormalized>#SUBJECT#<subjectId>
```

Isso está em [tracked-subject.ts:50](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/src/modules/subject/domain/tracked-subject.ts:50).

Logo:

- Com `type` obrigatório/conhecido: `begins_with(GSI7SK, "TYPE#x#NAME#prefix")` funciona.
- Sem `type`: não existe prefixo de nome utilizável; é necessário ler a partição de status e filtrar em memória.
- “Filtro por tipo” também precisa ser definido como key condition quando presente, não genericamente como filtro em memória.

A matriz e `searchSubjects` precisam distinguir explicitamente esses caminhos.

### 2. `ExpirationItem` não pode ser enriquecido com Subject

A Fatia 3 promete `BatchGetItem` de enriquecimento para “Requirement/Item” ([round2-claude-revision.md:210](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/docs/architecture/reviews/search-and-filters-scoping/round2-claude-revision.md:210)).

Entretanto, `ExpirationItem` não contém `subjectId`; seus campos estão em [expiration-item.ts:13](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/src/modules/expiration/domain/expiration-item.ts:13). Portanto, não existe chave física para resolver `ExpirationItem -> TrackedSubject`.

Além disso, a união retorna o item cru, sem `subjectDisplayName`, enquanto o texto anterior diz que hits de ambos os tipos seriam enriquecidos. É necessário escolher:

- somente `Requirement` recebe `subjectDisplayName`; ou
- adicionar uma relação real entre `ExpirationItem` e `TrackedSubject`, o que amplia significativamente o modelo e o protocolo.

### 3. A busca por status não define o universo quando `status` é omitido ou múltiplo

Cada status é uma partição distinta:

- Requirement: `REQSTATUS#<status>` em [requirement.ts:131](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/src/modules/document-archive/domain/requirement.ts:131).
- ExpirationItem: `ITEMSTATUS#<status>` em [expiration-item.ts:44](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/src/modules/expiration/domain/expiration-item.ts:44).
- Subject: `SUBJECTSTATUS#<status>` em [tracked-subject.ts:52](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/src/modules/subject/domain/tracked-subject.ts:52).

A revisão descreve uma Query por modo, mas não declara se `status` é obrigatório, qual é o default, nem se múltiplos status são permitidos. Sem status obrigatório, são necessárias várias Queries, múltiplos cursores e uma regra de composição — exatamente o problema que o redesenho pretende evitar.

Para manter cursor nativo simples, cada chamada deve exigir exatamente um status ou definir um único default inequívoco por modo.

### 4. Paginação ainda não possui contrato de página de resultado

O limite de cinco páginas DynamoDB resolve custo máximo, mas não define:

- quantidade máxima de hits devolvidos;
- se a leitura para ao atingir esse número ou sempre avalia até 125 itens;
- cursor quando o limite de hits é atingido no meio de uma página física;
- diferença entre `hasMore`, `scanLimitReached` e ausência definitiva de resultados;
- validação/namespace do cursor contra modo, tenant, status e ordenação;
- comportamento quando 125 itens avaliados produzem zero hits.

Há ainda uma contradição factual: até 125 itens podem produzir até 125 `subjectId`s distintos, portanto “um `BatchGetItem`”, “até 100 chaves” e “nunca perto do teto” não podem ser simultaneamente verdadeiros ([round2-claude-revision.md:78](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/docs/architecture/reviews/search-and-filters-scoping/round2-claude-revision.md:78)). O store existente já divide mais de 100 chaves em lotes em [dynamodb-subject-store.ts:128](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/src/modules/subject/persistence/dynamodb-subject-store.ts:128), mas isso deixa de ser “um único BatchGetItem”.

### 5. `matchedRequirements` introduz composição não especificada

O hit de Subject contém:

```ts
matchedRequirements?: RequirementSummary[]
```

Nenhuma decisão explica:

- quando esse campo aparece;
- como Requirements são buscadas;
- limite por Subject;
- paginação;
- custo máximo;
- significado de “matched” quando o modo Subject não aceita os mesmos filtros de Requirement.

Isso contradiz “único fan-out real” e o critério de completude física. Remova o campo desta fase ou forneça contrato físico completo.

### 6. Lookup de Requirements atribuídas não é uma Query

A revisão fala em `REQSTATUS#APPLICABLE`-equivalente, mas esse namespace não existe. `APPLICABLE` é `applicability`; as partições são pelos cinco valores de `RequirementStatus` ([requirement.ts:29](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/src/modules/document-archive/domain/requirement.ts:29), [requirement.ts:131](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/src/modules/document-archive/domain/requirement.ts:131)).

“Todo status exceto `NOT_APPLICABLE`” significa quatro Queries exaustivas:

- `MISSING`
- `PENDING`
- `SATISFIED`
- `NOT_SATISFIED`

Cada uma precisa percorrer todas as páginas antes de poder afirmar ausência. Isso precisa constar no custo e contrato da porta.

### 7. O fluxo de remoção não foi integrado de ponta a ponta

Adicionar um segundo método à porta não basta. Hoje cada serviço executa uma chamada e lança um erro com um único resultado:

- [remove-membership.ts:35](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/src/modules/organization/application/remove-membership.ts:35)
- [leave-organization.ts:31](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/src/modules/organization/application/leave-organization.ts:31)
- contrato atual em [assigned-active-items-lookup.ts:42](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/src/modules/organization/ports/assigned-active-items-lookup.ts:42).

A revisão deixa aberto “discriminador ou dois arrays”, mas isso determina:

- como os dois resultados são combinados;
- se o cap 20 é global ou por entidade;
- como `totalKnown` e `truncated` são calculados;
- se ambos os lookups sempre são executados;
- como o erro mantém compatibilidade tipada.

Essa escolha não pode ficar para implementação num design Type 1 que explicitamente promete mecanismo completo.

Também é inadequado importar diretamente a porta do módulo `expiration` no `document-archive`. Deve haver uma porta consumidora local ou um contrato compartilhado deliberadamente posicionado, com adapter no composition root.

### 8. O mapeamento de validade continua incompleto

A correção de `DRAFT` é boa, mas o design ainda não fornece uma tabela total dos adaptadores.

Faltam resultados explícitos para:

- Requirement `MISSING`;
- Requirement `PENDING`;
- Requirement `NOT_SATISFIED`;
- Requirement `NOT_APPLICABLE`;
- Requirement `SATISFIED` com e sem validade;
- ExpirationItem `ACTIVE`, `ARCHIVED`, `RENEWED` e `DELETED`;
- DocumentVersion `ACCEPTED` com e sem `validUntil`.

O código real distingue esses estados em [requirement.ts:161](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/src/modules/document-archive/domain/requirement.ts:161) e [expiration-item.ts:11](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/src/modules/expiration/domain/expiration-item.ts:11).

A assinatura genérica com `hasExpiry` e `isCurrentlyValid` ([round2-claude-revision.md:187](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/docs/architecture/reviews/search-and-filters-scoping/round2-claude-revision.md:187)) não resolve sozinha `AGUARDANDO_REVISAO`, inaplicabilidade ou estados terminais. O design deve apresentar uma tabela exhaustiva “estado de origem + datas → resultado ou exclusão”.

## Notas por critério

| Critério | Nota |
|---|---:|
| Completude física e custo de composição | 5,6 |
| Paginação e ordenação | 5,8 |
| Semântica de produto | 6,5 |
| Estado de validade unificado | 6,0 |
| Responsável | 6,1 |
| Governança de GSI | 9,0 |
| **Design ponderado** | **6,2/10** |

A governança de índices está substancialmente correta: GSI1 e GSI7 constam como tenant-facing em [main.tf:229](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/infra/modules/dynamo-table/main.tf:229), sem proposta de ampliar acesso aos índices restritos.

Para aprovação na Rodada 3, os oito pontos acima precisam virar contratos fechados, especialmente: status obrigatório/default por modo, paginação lógica completa, remoção de `matchedRequirements`, correção do enriquecimento de `ExpirationItem`, quatro partições de Requirement na reatribuição e tabela total de validade.
